#### aomac-only additions (non-retail, "Enhanced" page)

These rows are **not** part of the retail `OptionPanel/Root.xml` inventory above: performance audit item 2 (texture
anisotropy) and the MSAA-option decision (`docs/screens.md` "max anisotropy 1"; no AA option exists anywhere in the client's
`cd_image/gui`/`text`/`twk` data for either control), plus the windowing/camera Enhanced items 2, 6, 8 and 9, and the visual
options items 12, 13, 15 and 16 (`docs/gui.md` this section / `agent://EnhanceReview`). `options.rs::enhanced_page`
builds a synthetic [`Page`] (same `model::{Opt,Ctl,Item}` shapes the retail `Root.xml` parser produces) and appends it to the
category tree as a separate leaf labelled "Enhanced", reusing every generic page mechanism (tree
entry, XML control generation, value sync, click/slider dispatch) unmodified. Every control defaults to the retail value it
replaces, or to off/Auto for the ones with no retail equivalent, and persists through the same `DValues` → `Prefs.xml`
mechanism as `DisplayCompressedTextures` (`DV Main`, account-independent, survives restart).

The optional Sun Shadows (`AomacSunShadows`), Ambient Occlusion (`AomacAmbientOcclusion`),
Emissive Bloom (`AomacEmissiveBloom`) and Ground Grass (`AomacGroundGrass`) checkboxes are
independent DV Main booleans, all **off** by default, including when an older preferences
file lacks them. `DValues::enhanced_settings` maps them and the quality controls below to
`ao_render::EnhancedSettings`; `flow.rs::frame` sends the value through `Host.enhanced_settings`.
Both windowed and offscreen viewers drain that request into `Renderer::set_enhanced_settings`.
MSAA is independently selectable; there is no player-facing master/HDR switch.

| Quality control / DV Main key | Default | Bounds |
|---|---|---|
| Shadow Map Size / `AomacShadowMapSize` | 1024 | 256–4096 (UI choices 256/512/1024/2048/4096) |
| Ambient Occlusion Radius / `AomacAoRadius` | 1 m | 0–5 m |
| Ambient Occlusion Strength / `AomacAoStrength` | 1 | 0–1 |
| Emissive Bloom Strength / `AomacBloomStrength` | 0.6 | 0–1 |
| Ground Grass Density / `AomacGrassDensity` | 1 | 0–2 |
| Ground Grass Distance / `AomacGrassDistance` | 30 m | 0–35 m |

These are aomac-only artistic controls, not reconstructed retail settings. Values are bounded
at the renderer settings boundary; non-finite floats use the documented defaults.
**Restore Original** clears only the four effect flags via ordinary DValue writes, preserving
quality values, anisotropy, MSAA, camera and text preferences; the next frame returns to all-off
execution and the ordinary preference save persists it. Zero strength/density does not clear
an enabled flag (offline fixtures can explicitly request neutral Enhanced execution).

Enhanced explanations use native `TextView` multiline/word-wrap
(`TextRenderer_c::CalculatePreferredSize`, GUI `0x101623ea`), bounded by the widest Enhanced
control label in the normal font. Hidden explanatory prose therefore does not enlarge every
retail page. Source regressions cover all 16 flag combinations, account-independent persistence,
quality dispatch/reset preservation, and root width limits with and without the prose.
These tests are source coverage, not a claim of enabled-effect image or performance validation.

The renderer's optional boundary (`ao-render/enhanced.rs`, `enhanced_shader.rs`) keeps the
same native ordered world draws. Any enabled effect selects a colour attachment with the
**same gamma-space UNORM format as faithful**, plus a stored, sampleable depth attachment.
HDR/tonemapping is deliberately deferred: RGBA16F changed native per-draw saturation and
quantization (ICC neutral maximum error 41 byte levels; failed evidence retained under
`~/aomac-handoff/gfx/core-native-icc-neutral-1920`). Ambient-share and data-marked emission attachments
are independently allocated only for AO and bloom respectively, including their MSAA copies.
MSAA retains its native coverage; a minimum-sample depth resolve supplies single-sample
`Depth32Float` for postprocessing. The composite is an identity UNORM copy: existing world
shaders already output framebuffer-space colour, so applying an sRGB transfer or exposure
would be incorrect. It runs before native heat haze, viewport sprites and GUI. Shadows alter only the
attributable sun share in the Enhanced shader; AO subtracts only the ambient-share attachment,
not the whole image. Bloom consumes authored emission, never a brightness threshold.
All four flags off select the original shader/attachments and world pass with no optional
textures, composite, depth resolve or timer buffers; switching off releases the Enhanced frame.
The offline neutral profile is explicitly `ground_grass=true, grass_density=0`, not all-off.
Neutral execution preserves native blend semantics. The approved eleven-view comparison has
ten byte-exact views and one explicitly bounded exception: `icc-platform-authored`, 134 bytes
in 129 pixels, maximum one 8-bit level, mean absolute byte error 0.00001453993.
The fixture asserts only this named neutral view may differ by at most one level in at most
134 bytes; faithful and every other view still require exact equality. Independent repeats
are exact. The cause remains unresolved: sampled colour/stored depth differ from faithful
attachment usage; attributing the difference to those properties is an inference.
Grass has its own shader, so grass-only execution reuses the original world shader and cached
one-colour pipelines; this removes unnecessary variant work but did not change the known envelope.
`Renderer::set_gpu_timing(true)` explicitly allocates `FrameGpuTimer` only when timestamp queries
are supported; descriptor timestamps span the first actual skin/world pass through the last
presentation/heat-haze/viewport pass. Encoder timestamp writes can reorder on Metal and are not
used. Results resolve after the final pass and are mapped only after submission completion;
reversed counters are errors, never unsigned wraparound durations.
`read_frame_gpu_ms` reads each submitted frame before timer reuse.
This synchronous instrumentation is offline-only and disabled by default.

Measured headlessly on Apple M4 Pro (20 GPU cores), 1920×1200, 1× MSAA:
the ten-view all-off cutover is byte-identical to the pre-Enhanced baseline
(`~/aomac-handoff/gfx/core-faithful-cutover-1920`, 92,160,000 compared bytes).
The corrected native ICC neutral image is also byte-exact; its explicit ABBA benchmark
(120 warmups + 600 samples per profile) measured off/neutral GPU p50
0.500875/0.541708 ms and CPU encode-submit p50 0.576000/0.592125 ms.
Raw samples, PNG/RGBA, source patch and environment limitations are retained in
`~/aomac-handoff/gfx/core-native-icc-unorm-completed-1920`.
This is a boundary comparison under shared-machine load, not combined-effect or controlled
thermal/power acceptance. Reproduce with ignored test
`native_icc_neutral_pixels_and_gpu_timing` and a new `AOMAC_GFX_CORE_OUT` directory.
The original encoder-timestamp numbers are invalid and retained only as failed evidence;
deferred GPU counter resolution fixes the reproduced incomplete-counter ordering problem
([wgpu #9414](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9414)).

| aomac page / control | Key → store | Runtime consumer | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Enhanced` / `Texture Sharpening (anisotropic filtering)` | `AomacTextureSharpening` → DV Main (index 0-4) | `flow.rs::frame` maps the index to 1/2/4/8/16 → `Host.anisotropy` → `Renderer::set_anisotropy` rebuilds the world + clamp-address samplers; default index 0 = 1x, matching retail `FUN_10041ede`/`docs/screens.md` "max anisotropy 1" (audit item 2) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Edge Smoothing (MSAA)` | `AomacEdgeSmoothing` → DV Main (index 0-3) | `flow.rs::frame` maps the index to 1/2/4/8 samples → `Host.msaa_samples` → `Renderer::set_msaa_samples` downward-clamps to what the adapter's colour+depth format features support (`clamp_msaa_samples`) and rebuilds every pipeline; default index 0 = 1 sample (off), matching retail (no AA option found anywhere in the client). Alpha-tested (cutout) pipelines also pick up `alpha_to_coverage_enabled` once MSAA > 1 (audit item 4: `lib.rs::build_pipelines`, `shader.wgsl`'s `shade`/`shade_sky`), smoothing foliage/fence/hair edges; off whenever MSAA is off, matching retail's hard cutout exactly | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Interface Scale` | `AomacInterfaceScale` → DV Main (index 0-3: Auto/1x/2x/3x) | Read at `Play::new` before the login window, refreshed live from HUD DValues and reapplied through `Host.interface_scale` → `viewer.rs::gui_scale`. Explicit 1x/2x/3x choices are honored without a viewport-size floor; Auto rounds the native backing scale. The removed 1024×768 floor silently forced both 2x and 3x to 1x on a 5120×1440 window (720/480 logical height) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Instant Fullscreen` (checkbox) | `AomacBorderlessFullscreen` → DV Main (bool) | `flow.rs::frame` → `Host.borderless_game`; read by `viewer.rs`'s Ctrl+Cmd+F handler (`WindowExtMacOS::set_borderless_game`) when entering fullscreen, skipping the Spaces animation and hiding the menu bar/dock. Default off (today's animated native fullscreen). Fullscreen state itself (on/off, which monitor) is remembered across restarts in `Window.cfg` regardless of this setting (audit item 6) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Pinch to Zoom` (checkbox) | `AomacPinchZoom` → DV Main (bool) | `flow.rs::frame` → `Host.pinch_zoom`; `viewer.rs` forwards `WindowEvent::PinchGesture` as `GameInput::Pinch` only while set, `player.rs::game_input` feeds it through the same `Controls::on_wheel`/camera-zoom path as the mouse wheel (same 0.78-25 m range). Default off, opt-in (audit item 8) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Chat Font Size` (slider) | writes the *retail* `ChatFontSize` (`MainPrefs.xml` template, tenths of a point, range 50-480) | `chat/win.rs::WinPrefs::from_dvalues` already reads `ChatFontSize` live; this is the on-screen control retail never shipped (`/setoption ChatFontSize 180` only). Window rebuild on change, like any other chat preference (audit item 9) | **AOMAC-ONLY control on a retail variable, LIVE** |
| `Enhanced` / `Better Mip Filtering` | `AomacBetterMipFiltering` → DV Main (bool) | `flow.rs::frame` reads the flag into `Host.texture_policy`'s third field → `Renderer::set_texture_policy` → `native_texture::Policy::better`, consumed by `build_texture_mips` (audit item 12): gamma-correct (linear-light) RGB downsample instead of averaging sRGB bytes directly, plus coverage-preserving alpha rescaling for every texture used by a `Blend::AlphaTest` submesh (foliage, fences, hair cards), so distant mips keep the base level's alpha-test coverage instead of thinning out. Default off = retail's plain sRGB box filter (randy31 `FUN_100193e4`); only affects textures built at the next zone/model load (CPU-side only, same GPU byte layout either way) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE at next load; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Widescreen FOV` | `AomacWidescreenFOV` → DV Main (bool) | `flow.rs::frame` reads the flag into `Player::set_widescreen_fov` → `camera::lens` picks a vertical-locked lens at `FOV_VERTICAL_WIDE` (73.7398°, the retail 4:3 lens' vertical angle) instead of the retail fixed 90° horizontal; also feeds `Dynels::fov_horizontal` (via `camera::horizontal_fov`) so `dynels.rs`'s perf pre-skin animation cull widens along with the real lens instead of culling edge characters the renderer still draws (audit item 16 prerequisite). Horizontal view becomes 100° at 16:10, 106° at 16:9, 121° at 21:9. PRK-signed-off (`agent://EnhanceReview` "widescreen FOV"); default off = retail's fixed 90° (N3 `n3Camera_t` ctor) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Extended Scenery Distance` | `AomacSceneryDistance` → DV Main (float slider, 1.0-2.0x) | `flow.rs::frame` reads the value into `Player::set_scenery_distance_mult`, multiplying the far plane `camera::far_plane(view_distance)` already derives from the retail `ViewDistance` pref (audit item 15); `Renderer::set_lens` already moves the fog end and statel LOD view length with the far plane, so terrain/statel draw distance, fog and LOD extend together. Never touches `Dynels::char_view_distance` (still capped at `DisplayCharViewDistance`, max 80 m) or the 30 m `nametag.rs` radius, which use their own distance checks unrelated to the camera far plane. Known, bounded risk left unmitigated (ponytail: standard, non-reversed `Mat4::perspective_rh` depth precision; retail already reaches a 5000:1 near/far ratio at `ViewDistance` 1.0, this option only doubles it to 10000:1 at most, and the affected content is always far from the camera, where z-fighting is not a practical concern — upgrade to reversed-Z if a real artifact is observed) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |
| `Enhanced` / `Anti-aliased Text` | `AomacSmoothText` → DV Main (bool) | Read at startup and refreshed live; `flow.rs::frame` always applies the smooth flag and `Host.window_scale` to `Gui::set_text_scale`, including login/character selection without a HUD. TrueType glyphs use `Flatten::fill_aa` 4×4 coverage at their actual physical raster scale, including 1x; the smooth flag is independent of scale. Advance/line height remain retail-hinted. Changing mode resets the reusable glyph atlas and map together, preventing stale coverage and accumulated scale variants. The nearest sampler remains 1:1. Bitmap fonts (Tooltip, Shell, Clock) stay retail pixel art; Chat is TrueType and is smoothed. Default off reproduces retail 1-bit glyphs (`GUI.dll` 0x1012ec5c / 0x1012e73c) | **AOMAC-ONLY, LIVE; not a retail control** |

Window-level restore (not an Enhanced-page control): `ao-render/window_prefs.rs`'s `Window.cfg` also remembers which display the
window was on (by name, since display IDs are not stable across reboots) and whether it was fullscreen (audit items 6/7).
`viewer.rs::State::new` places the window back on that monitor if it is still connected (else the primary one) and clamps the
saved position fully onto it (`window_prefs::clamp_to_monitor`), so a display unplugged/resized/rearranged since the last run
never opens the window off-screen. Ctrl+Cmd+F toggles fullscreen instantly (`winit::window::Fullscreen::Borderless`) in addition
to the green button's native fullscreen.

### Action-menu click dispatch and character-local chat archives

The GUI identity of an action-menu entry is distinct from its optional DValue name. The unnamed
`#Help/se` submenu in `ActionMenu/CommandMenu.xml:21` references `Windows.xml`; its Settings entry
(`Windows.xml:5`, `optionpanel_window`) must remain clickable through both popup levels. Popup
placement captures the clicked entry's screen rectangle before replacing its owner popup
(`FUN_100653b6` geometry), rather than looking for every entry in the control-centre view.
`Actions` retain their source order, event and criteria: `RunChatScript` routes through the same
chat command path as typed input (`PetMenu.xml:5–26`), while `SetValue` and `CloseMenu` handle
`CommandMenu.xml:14–16` and `MapMenu.xml:3–11`.
Action categories use each registered template's actual stat 588, not a guessed Action_e split.
`ItemTemplate action_name` compares the template name case-insensitively (`GUI 0x10070c04`);
`active_item_count` counts criteria-enabled children (`0x100706d1`). The XML Invite override
matches RDB 1000020:82216 (`Invite Person to Your Team`, category 2), but no original base
SA registry mapping or `PerformSpecialAction` invite branch has been established
(`GC 0x100428ad`, `0x10043005`, `0x1004256c`). It is not made artificially always-visible:
the proven selected-target invitation path is Team Recruit (`GUI 0x10077c8e`, called by
`0x10077fb3`).
Camera `invoke`/`active_value` entries use the original constants first-person 0, free 1,
rubber 2, locked 3 (`GUI 0x10064631`, active comparison `0x1006531c`). The GUI command queue
changes the actual `Camera3p` vehicle, not just its DValue; preference synchronization follows
`0x10064283`/`0x10064387` (first-person clears third-person without losing its preferred vehicle).

Windowed verification used muted `aomac play` and CGEvent title/edge drags. At 1280×800, a
fresh character preference directory showed framed `Default Window [Vicinity]`/`Combat`, a dark body and input line;
Wear showed all four tabs inside its strip and Stats bar text was centered. The real
Actions → Help/Settings → Settings clicks opened Preferences/Fixed keys/Key bindings.
Resizing to 1000×650 preserved the right rollup anchor. Wear undocked and redocked through
real title drags, chat tabs tore out, and Team moved/resized. The first frame exposed the
shared zero-width dock-body bug (§6.5); a subsequent real run after that fix restored the
saved Team position/height and kept all six rows inside its frame at both sizes.

Chat window archives belong to the selected account's `Char<ID>/Chat/Windows`, not the shared
application preferences root. Legacy port-created shared archives migrate once into the first
character without existing archives; an existing character's custom mode, groups, opacity and
positions take precedence. Retail `NewChar/Chat/Windows/Window1/Config.xml:57–64` sets visual mode
2, inactive/active opacity 0.3/0.8 and an enabled input line; line 74 names it `Default Window`.
Mode 0 is the framed, unfaded tabbed presentation (`FUN_10096ec5`); the shipped mode 2 is a
borderless black-panel presentation. Window alpha reaches the background/chrome via
`Window::UpdateFadeLevel` `10155653`, but chat output/editor/prompt renderers set view flag
`0x80` (`100925ff`, `100919ab`), making `View::_CallRender` `1014d2e3` reset inherited alpha.
The text does **not** fade with the window; the hint retains its own 0.6. See `docs/chat/gui.md` §2.
For a fresh character the port deliberately selects mode 0 instead of the shipped mode-2 template,
as requested to match the user's framed retail reference. Saved character modes are not overridden.

The live walk harness accepts `resize=WxH`, replacing only its render targets and preserving the
frontend/window state, so dock anchoring can be exercised at several surface sizes in one session.

### Grouped dock persistence

`DockingController` writes one `DockAreas/<name>.xml` per group, with ordered
`docked_view_identities`, `selected_tab`, frame/pin, `dock_node_configs` and `scroll_offset`.
Character groups overlay the templates; closed identities remain in the group for later opening.
Singleton window saves do not replace shared dock frames. Known view archives retain unrelated
children while updating `DockableViewDockName`. Inventory/actions use grouped identities as the
authority because their exact view-archive keys have not been established; no keys are invented.
Evidence: controller `0x1003a0a1`/`0x1003a5e6`, dock destructor `0x1003b672`,
retail `NewChar/DockAreas/RollupArea.xml:3–30` node/identity arrays and `Prefs.xml` Friends
73–78, NCU 505–553, Nano 583–584, Stat 905–911, Team 914–915, Wear 926–929.

Friends is a `DockableView` (`0x100a9c58` → base `0x10038b47`, vtable confirmed) and is
registered with the HUD dock controller through the allocation-free `Chat::dock_windows`
iterator. LFT is a plain movable/resizable Window (`0x100f03fb`, style 0, flags 0x1000)
with `LoadWndConfig` and destructor `0x100efb97` `SaveWndConfig`. Social content/frame/pin
archives are character-local. Chat's close button now routes `CloseRequested` to the window
owner; closed documents retain history/configuration and reopen through Enter.

### Native XML Slider

`ao-gui` now parses `Slider` directly, sharing the options window's native slider implementation instead of application-painted canvas controls. GUI XML constructor `0x1014469d` reads float `min_value`, `max_value`, `default_value` (all default 0), integer `gfxid_slider` / `gfxid_background` (zero selects 0x91 / 0x92). It has no increment/step attribute: `MouseMove` supplies a continuous double; consumer slots implement any integer snapping. The constructor's default value is stored directly; subsequent `SetValue` (`0x10144277`) clamps, ignores unchanged values, and emits only when notify is true. The public observer setter is silent.

Preferred sizing `0x101440c4` uses the knob's inclusive width/height, background border insets (zero for the background-only set), and maximum width 16000 (`float` at `0x101c785c`). `_Layout` `0x10144141` floors the normalized horizontal offset and vertically centers the knob. Art remains skin IDs 0x92 background / 0x91 DEFAULT-tinted handle (11×18). `MouseDown` `0x10143efc` captures only a handle press; `MouseMove` `0x10143f8a` retains the grab offset and emits `(value,false)` while captured, including outside the view; `MouseUp` `0x101443b3` emits `(value,true)` even without a value change and releases capture. `Event::SliderChanged` carries that native final-change flag. `native_xml_slider_set_range_drag_and_signal` covers silent setters, range clamp, unchanged moves, captured drag/release, and native handle paint coordinates.

### RadioButton composed View labels

Research row construction `GUI.dll 0x100f5de8` passes `0x100f4d9c`'s Powerbar/TextView horizontal View to the `RadioButton_c` View-pointer constructor `0x10140a40`. That constructor calls `_Initialize` (`0x1014067a`), removes the generated TextView label, appends the supplied View, and adds a zero-weight horizontal spacer. Unlike the generated text label, the supplied View receives no extra label border. The existing marker metrics remain unchanged; composed content starts immediately after its 11-pixel marker. The retail XML constructor `0x10140ba0` itself uses a string label; our nested XML View is the serialization of the runtime View-pointer constructor, not a claim that retail XML replaces the label.

`ao-gui` retains nested RadioButton content, includes its preferred dimensions, lays it out after the marker, draws the native Powerbar/TextView children, and routes child hits to the enclosing radio's existing selection/click path. `native_radio_view_label_paints_and_selects` covers child dimensions, glyph/bar painting, and selection through either child. No label-only radio sizing or marker drawing metrics changed.

### Native Button background icons

`set_button_background_icon` retains and paints the actual bitmap, independently of raised/pressed/hover border art. `Button_c::SetBackgroundIcon` (`GUI.dll 0x10127d08`) uses skin art when its bool is false, or `ViewSurface_c::Load(id,0x8f)` when true; vehicle construction `0x100f3259` supplies itemstat 79 and true. The application resolves that RDB PNG through the existing `hud_bar::icon_image` loader and supplies its registered GfxId.

`LayoutLabel` (`0x101278d6`) centers the bitmap at its own size without pressed displacement. Mode-0 `CalculatePreferredSize` (`0x10127ee4`) takes maximum label/icon content dimensions before Rect(8,1,8,4), 10-pixel horizontal and 2-pixel vertical padding. `StateChanged` (`0x10128338`) multiplies every child by white or disabled 0x909090. Bitmap alpha is layer 2. `UpdateBgIconFade` (`0x10127bdd`) uses grey `32 + trunc((1-fade)*223)` and shows the label only above 0.8; `MouseMove` (`0x10127869`) starts 30000-us ticks, each changing fade by 0.075 (`SlotFadeTimer`, `0x10127c6d`). CCMenuEntry XML bgicon shares the bitmap painter/fade timer. Native Button XML constructor `0x10128c73` does not read bgicon; only CCMenuEntry (`0x100660f2`) supports that attribute, so no invented Button XML attribute is added.

Regression coverage: `native_background_icon_bounds_and_states` and `native_background_icon_timer_ticks`.

### Native Research and Vehicle windows

`hud_research_vehicle.rs` opens the shipped `Research.xml`, `ResearchDetails.xml`, `ResearchFinished.xml`, and `MechSelect.xml`. Native constructors are GUI `0x100f6bf4` / `0x100f3566`; settings are `ResearchConfig` / `MechSelectConfig`, with `esc_research` / `esc_vehicle` captured by the common HUD per-open escape policy. The authored `HelloKittyMech` designer sample is removed, as retail `0x100f3259` does; vehicles are only populated from genuinely completed global goals, never synthetic entries.

The supplied offline frames `offline-research-{allocate,available,finished}.png` were inspected: allocation was internally contained but all tabs were truncated; Available showed seven full cyan bars and its selected-line heading escaped the right edge; Finished let the two column headings run together. These were real port mismatches, not a reason to invent a larger fixed window. Constructor `0x100f6bf4` uses Rect(200,180,850,700), then centers and loads `ResearchConfig`; shipped `CharPrefs.xml` supplies an empty ResearchConfig archive, not a smaller default rectangle. The shared ViewSelector first-page preferred-size anchor incorrectly clamped every tab to Allocate's maximum. Native `0x1014fd77/0x1014fe68` instead measures all three pages componentwise, so the shipped Details/Finished heading minima now participate in the window limits.

Native row builder `0x100f4d9c` explicitly calls Powerbar SetValue with `N3Msg_GetPerkProgress`, before `0x100f5de8` wraps the row in a radio button. The port's XML `value` attribute was ignored by PowerBar construction, leaving its default 1.0. Every research row now uses the existing progress setter after insertion. Native `PowerbarView_c::Recalculate` `0x1013e789` floors right = left + width × fraction; zero therefore retains the inclusive one-pixel baseline rather than a full bar. Constructor `0x100f6bf4` also overrides the finished `info` renderer to min(0,20)/max(16000,16000), now applied rather than retaining the XML's 80px minimum. The existing offline fixture checks seven zero-fraction draw widths, real heading text widths/rectangles and column containment on all three pages. No checks or new screenshots were executed for this correction; parent verification is required.

The existing rdb `1000036` perk catalogue supplies project identifiers, precursors, flags, item templates, and word 5 research totals. Item rdb `1000020` supplies authored names/descriptions and vehicle icons (stat 79). `0x100f4d9c` builds horizontal progress rows with graphic ids `0xe0/0xe1`, borders `(8,2,0,2)`, label, and spacer; available projects use the native composed radio control. `0x1002ba03` scales global totals by integer 1000; `0x1002bc59` initializes an existing negative global timer to that total, while absent server entries remain absent. Research sliders dispatch SetStat 263/264 and constrain their sum to 100; selected projects dispatch 265/266. The real ResearchRequestIIR/ResearchUpdateIIR and SpawnMechIIR serializers drive requests and updates.

Mech activation follows GC `0x10079707`: Locks_t skill-662 membership, outdoor playfield, own Vehicle_t `+0x50` falling enabled, vehicle-allowed resource flags, resurrection shock stat 247, and (for catalogue flag `0x100`) battlestation or the own-position authored land-control district. N3 `0x1001c115` reads blob offset 52 into resource `+0x50`, and offset 60 into `+0x58`; GC `0x10121906` identifies Shadowlands with `+0x50 & 2`, and `0x10121915` also forbids `+0x58 & 0x01000000`. Battlestation identities are `0x111c..0x1125` (exclusive upper bound). District data comes from the existing native FightLevels zone query, not an invented server ownership flag. Refusals use the authored system feedback texts. Native GUI `0x100f3017` records `mech_ordered` from synchronized `GameTime+0xb4` (`0x10016b9b`) and closes the window after the activation attempt; the leave action also closes.

Real server ResearchUpdate/full-character/perk payloads are prerequisites for completed global vehicles and personal progression. Missing payloads do not manufacture completed research, permit a missing activation predicate, or render a placeholder panel. The native catalogue/progress regression is `native_goal_availability_progress_and_global_sides`; shared eligibility, slider, composed-radio, icon, resource-flag, lock, and activation-guard regressions cover their respective native consumers. No live session, screenshots, or checks were run by this implementation worker.

### Native tower build menu and guarded city terminal policy

`interact_tower.rs` implements `TowerBuildMenuView_c` (GUI `0x1005c35b`) from its code-built controls, native 409×369 dimensions, RDB item icon/name, LDB-503 checklist labels and `/showfile` links, and the exact controller/tower/shield-disabler failure masks initialized at `0x1005a723`. Inventory/hotbar activation and world item use intercept the native interface path before ordinary use validation: Interfaces `0x1000a707` tests TowerType stat `0x184`, then GC `0x100274e1` identifies shield-disablers through use-event criterion action `0x53`. Opening captures `esc_towerbuildmenu`; later preference changes affect the next constructor, not the existing instance. The frame button always closes. `TowerBuildMenuViewConfig` uses the existing window archive geometry/pin persistence.

Check sends the real CharacterAction `0xaf`, with `(low_id, high_id)` in identity A and `(level, 0)` in identity B (GC `0x100294a7`). The own-character `0xb0` response reaches the native checklist flags; Build remains disabled until the selected item is owned and the displayed predicates pass. GUI `0x1005a1ed` compares all three coordinates of the **own global character position** exactly and disables Build after any change. This is the retail placement locus, not a cursor-ground-ray substitute. GUI `0x1005a242` calls `BuildTower`; Interfaces `0x10008276` directly invokes `Gamecode::N3Msg_UseItem(item, false)` through IAT `0x10015444`, so the existing sequence-numbered GenericCmd-3 serializer commits the real build.

Signal `GlobalSignals +0x160` is **not evidence of a world preview ghost**. The native GUI connection at `0x10043225` targets `ItemIconView` handler `0x100425c2`, which only clears the matching icon identity/selection flag; selecting the tower at `0x1005be1b` and destroying its menu at `0x1005a2f1` emit that signal. No invented ghost geometry, collision check, coordinate payload, or success reply is introduced. The request contract regression is `checklist_request_preserves_all_acg_words`.

City terminal is genuinely service-data-gated: `CityTerminalGUIModule_c::SlotOpenCityTerminal` (`GUI 0x100e630b`, re-entry `0x100e63fe`) requires `PlayfieldCityHolderClient::GetPFCityHolderClient` and `CityClientInterface::HasCityInterface`, then an actual city model of kind 1 or 2 before constructing `CityTerminalView` (`0x100e6f11`; ESC read at `0x100e723c`). The guarded `Interact::city_terminal_constructor_policy` captures `esc_cityterminal` only for those model kinds; it is not read per frame and no generic item-use callback is invented as a replacement city service signal. The missing prerequisite is the native `city.dll` CityClientInterface city-model payload/lifecycle and its OpenGUISession signal emitter. This client currently has neither a decoded city-service model nor a captured city session supplying that input. The shipped `Views/CityTerminal.xml` alone is not sufficient to reconstruct upkeep, bank, construction, demolition, cloaking, and organization authorization state, so no empty terminal shell is opened.

No build, test, linter, live connection, or screenshot verification was run by this worker.
### Running NCU cancellation (bug 21)

`HudNcu` routes a running row's right click, as well as double click, to cancellation.
GUI `FUN_10044c31` only calls `N3Msg_RemoveBuff` for the own NCU view
(`+0x370/+0x374` both zero), printing “Deactivating friendly timed nanoprogram.”
on success and “Can't remove hostile timed nanoprogram.” on rejection.
Gamecode `N3Msg_RemoveBuff` `0x1001be60` rejects `Flags & 0xc100`, then sends
`CharacterActionIIR_t` action `0x41`, param zero, identity A `{0,0}`,
identity B `{0xcf1b,nano_id}`, empty text, under the controlled character identity
through `FUN_1007253f` and `SendIIRToObservers`. The existing byte encoder matches
this layout. Requesting cancellation does not remove a local row or modify NCU:
server `BuffIIR` kind zero removes the lifecycle entry (`GC 0x10050afd`).
Regressions cover hostile bits, decoded request identities, right-click feedback,
retaining the row until server removal, and refreshing afterward. Headless
read-only GUI/Gamecode decompilation supplied this evidence; tests/live gates
are owned by the parent and were not run here.

Running NCU is not a hotbar drag source in retail: `NCUListView_c` constructor
`0x10045354` connects info at `+0x2e4` (`0x10045405`), tooltip at `+0x140`
(`0x10045418`), cancellation at `+0x2e0` (`0x1004542b`), and context menu at
`+0x2e8` (`0x1004543e`); neither it nor `NCUView_c` constructor `0x100d6dd6`
connects the base `+0x2d8` BeginDrag signal to a payload producer. Programs
explicitly connects that signal to `0x100d3807`. Native NCU rows therefore do
not produce shortcut payloads; Programs rows do. The cancellation regression
selects an installed timed template whose Flags pass the retail friendly mask,
rather than assuming the existing duration-only fixture is friendly.

### Own-character world selection and shift inspection (bugs 17 / 10)

The own avatar now supplies its current posed body box to the shared world picker
in third person, and clears it in first person. This follows N3 `FUN_10020a3c`:
the client character is eligible when `camera+0x1ec` is set (see `hud_pick.rs`).
Previously `Dynels::pick_bodies` unconditionally excluded it despite that evidence.
The box comes from the same CPU-skinned frame submitted for drawing, with the
avatar's live position, heading and body scale, not the remote own-character pose.

Read-only GUI decompilation of `FUN_1002c469` confirms left release checks character
attack qualifiers `&0xc` first, then inspect qualifiers `&3`: character inspection
calls `ShowURL("charid://50000/id")`; other dynels use `itemid://kind/id`, without
changing selection. The port now preserves that precedence when both are held.
Existing HUD-to-InfoView routing handles players, NPCs and world object identities.
Regressions cover own-body ray selection, inspection leaving selection unchanged,
first-person exclusion, and mixed modifier precedence. No build/test/live gate was
run by this worker; the parent owns those gates.

### Item activation and inspection (bug 10)

Inventory/wear grid and list pointer input now shares `use_slot`: right press
wears/uses a bag item or moves a worn item back to the bag; modifier-left requests
InfoView only and clears the drag press and double-click history. Bank and loot
canvas presses request the existing server-authoritative take path on right;
modifier-left opens the item's identity URL and suppresses the release action.

Native evidence: `FUN_10040a17` tests left qualifiers `&3` and emits list signal
`+0x2e4` (assembly `10040ae4`), connected by `FUN_100cc1b3` at `100cc2ae`
to `FUN_100c9afc` (`InfoViewModule::ShowURL`). The legacy right branch
`10040b31 -> 10040af5` emits `+0x2e0`, connected at `100cc278` to
`FUN_100ca1e7`: external container (`+0x14c == 0`) or bank (`+0x140 == 0xdead`)
calls `MoveItemToInventory`; owned inventory calls `N3Msg_UseItem(false)`.
The new-mouse right branch `10040acc` emits `+0x2e8` through `FUN_10041c66`;
`100cc293` connects this to `FUN_100ca2d3`, a popup menu, not direct inspection.
The requested observed right-use behavior is retained without inventing a
mouse-mode setting. Regressions cover inventory grid/list, worn items, bank and
loot right activation and modifier-left inspection. Worker did not run gates.

### Team header and title-drag docking (2026-10-15)

`TeamView_c` constructor GUI.dll `0x100785f0` creates its header as a NORMAL
TextView with View flags `5` and text feature flags `0`: single-line, not
word-wrapped. It sets `TextRenderer::SetMaxPreferredSize(16000,-1)` (floats at
`0x101b65e8` / `0x101a9264`) and places it inside borders `(4,2,4,2)`.
The port omitted the header's clip flag and ignored View flag `1` in drawing.
`View::_CallRender` `0x1014d2e3` intersects every node's screen bounds with its
inherited bounds; flag `1` applies that intersection only while rendering that
view, restoring the previous viewport afterward. The renderer now carries that
intersection through recursion and honors the flag; Team retains its retail
single line without painting beyond the client.

Team is a regular `DockableView` (`0x10038b47`, invoked by `0x100785f0`), not a
no-docking window: constructor argument `5` is the View flag mask, not a docking
restriction. `prefs/NewChar/DockAreas/DockArea1.xml` explicitly stores `team_view`
in a `DockTabbedWindow`. The port's blank-title movement path only armed a frame
drag; unlike pressing the tab itself, it never emitted `TabDropped` on release.
Sole-tab movable dock titles now track their tab drop as well as moving the
frame. **[INFERENCE / unresolved gesture parity]** forwarding blank-title moves
to the tab drop is an input-equivalence choice: `DockWindow_c` `0x1003bdd4`
connects the TabView drag signal to `0x1003ba71`, which creates the native
`dockableview/view` drag. That establishes tab docking, not blank-title docking.
Resize edges retain their separate gesture. Docked windows temporarily disable
free-window preferred-size limits so a live long header cannot widen the
controller-owned 192-pixel page; undocking restores those limits.
`DockWindow` drop handler `0x1003bbf2` cancels a drop onto the same window when it
has one tab. The port mirrors this guard so moving a free window preserves its
saved `selected_tab = -1` sentinel instead of rewriting it to `0`.

Options uses retail style `0`, flags `0x1000` (`OptionWindow_c` `0x100c3c44`),
without either no-resize bit (`0x10` / `0x20`). The existing port already enables
frame resizing and content-derived limits. New regressions exercise raw corner
drag, clamping, category selection and saved-frame restore, including the full
HUD update/event path, rather than assuming the owner flag alone proves it.
The visible non-retail page label is now simply `Enhanced`.

**Verification (2026-10-15):** `cargo test --release --workspace -j 2` passed
1822 tests (60 ignored), and release workspace/all-target Clippy passed with
`-D warnings`. Team long-header clipping/containment, actual pointer docking,
Options border dragging through full HUD updates, scale choices at exactly
5120×1440, AA coverage at 1x and login-without-HUD application are covered.

Capture-only real-window checks used three muted, isolated login-screen
sessions, each under 30 seconds, one owned PID at a time, with the live lock and
3 GB RSS watchdog. No account login or keyboard/mouse input was used.
The Odyssey G93SC reports a 5120×1440 display at backing scale **1.0**;
macOS constrained the window's outer bounds to 5120×1356. Window-only captures
were 5232×1468 including shadows. At the same native window size, the login
frame's cyan bounds doubled exactly from 354×129 at Interface Scale 1x to
708×258 at 2x. At 1x, enabling smooth text changed 107 pixels in the 75×30
Username-label crop from binary edges to intermediate coverage, while those
frame bounds stayed identical. Captures were inspected and deleted; every
owned app exited with code 0 before lock release. Real in-game interactive
Options resize, live setting toggles and Team drag checks remain unexercised:
desktop input was withdrawn for this session, so their evidence is offscreen
regression coverage rather than a real-window interaction claim.

### Progression and abilities: offline acceptance (2026-10-16)

`progression_offline_window_shots` passed with the installed client data and
offscreen renderer. Six 1280×800 fixture frames were inspected: native perk
status icons and the plain green alien-perk label; full Skills; expanded
compact Skills with six distinct, nonoverlapping ability rows; and Research's
allocation, available and finished pages. The latter retain zero progress and
contain their headings and complete tabs at the native all-pages preferred
size. These are explicitly offline fixtures, not server-earned progression.

The frame inspection exposed and corrected three concrete defects that
state-only checks missed: literal alien-label `<font>` markup, compact rows
overlaid in an empty BorderView, and Research's unassigned Powerbar values
plus first-page-only ViewSelector sizing. Their native sources and regression
assertions are recorded in the feature sections above.

Verification used the private release target, `RUST_TEST_THREADS=2`, muted
audio and an isolated preferences directory. After rebasing, `cargo test
--release --workspace -j 2` passed **2035 tests across 40 suites, with 63
ignored**. Subsequent mechanical removal of obsolete low-ID item metadata
and migration of test-only diagnostics to the canonical ACG cache passed all
**62** affected `play::hud_stats::` tests (`cargo test --release -p aomac
--bin aomac -j 2 play::hud_stats::`). Strict
`cargo clippy --release --workspace --all-targets -j 2 -- -D warnings` passed.
The inspected fixture PNGs were deleted; no desktop capture or real window
was used.

Live acceptance is blocked by the user's PRK maintenance instruction.
No login, leveling, character creation, perk train/reset, implant clinic
session, combat nano cast or owned-pet command session was attempted in this
sweep. Those server interactions must not be inferred from fixture results.

### Knowledge references (`play/hud_knowledge.rs`)

GUI `KnowledgeView_c` **0x100cefe4** constructs `knowledge_window`, title
`Knowledge`, initially in `RollupArea`, and a plain `ItemListViewBase` with no
added columns. `0x100d694e` / float **0x101bd710=167** supplies its initial
inclusive extent. `MultiListView_c` **0x10136423** supplies icon extent47
(48 pixels), borders6 (**0x101b5234**), spacing10 (**0x101a98e4**); the
existing inventory grid arithmetic is reused. Unbounded48px grid background
selection is **0x10134023**, art **0x11d**.

Reference add/remove callbacks are **0x100ce591/0x100ce6f7**. Item references
retain low/high/QL and inspect via the existing `itemref://` InfoView path;
character references retain the complete dated HTML snapshot and use
`charref://kind/id/html` (**0x100cea17**, also context callback **0x100ceafb**).
The native item dummy allocator **GC0x10082a3d** uses identity kind **0xc788**,
monotonic instances and deduplicated low/high/QL triples. Character icon
selection is **GUI0x1003eb30**, art **0xb8**; missing item icons use native
**0xb9**. Reference type3 adds badge **0x15c** (**0x1003f859**), placed bottom-left
and scaled against48 pixels (**0x1003f2ea**, double **0x101b03f8=48**).

Item drops copy reference data without moving inventory
(**0x100ce811**); own-reference drags move only into empty cells, do not swap,
and are rejected by shortcut bars (**0x100d8047**). Native drag cursor offset
is16 (**0x100ce74e**, float **0x101b55e4**). The context menu
**0x100cee16** reads `Look@Item` (**0x101bdf94**) and `DeleteReference`
(**0x101bdf84**); delete calls **0x100ce9dc**. Qualified clicks inspect on
release through the existing item-list info signal; double-click also inspects.

`KnowledgeViewConfig` contains ordered `item_id` / `item_pos` pairs
(save **0x100cebe2**, restore **0x100cecf1**); restore stops using saved
positions after the first identity mismatch. The view participates in the
shared Rollup/DockAreas persistence. `Preferences_t::OpenGamePrefs`
**0x101248ff/0x10124971** initializes References with the character directory
and load limit **20**; `InventoryGUIModule::SlotInitialize` **0x100c722f**
loads it. Binary storage evidence and malformed-input checks are in
`docs/formats.md`. Failed loads cannot overwrite an existing reference file.
The native `OpenGamePrefs` creates the character directory before reference
initialization (`CreateDirectoryA`); the reference save follows the existing
shortcut preference convention and creates that directory before its first
write. This matters for a new character whose other preferences have not
yet been flushed. Item reference labels and icons resolve both ACG template
endpoints at the saved quality level through the existing metadata cache.

Regression `native_reference_grid_inspects_moves_deletes_and_restores` uses
only in-memory GUI input and private preference files: native icon/badge art,
release inspection, item-copy insertion, reference movement, immutable live
dummy identities after deletion, snapshot/config reload, and preservation
of a malformed file.

Headless acceptance (2026-10-16): `cargo test --release --workspace -j 2`
passed **2082 tests across 40 suites, 63 ignored**; `cargo clippy --release
--workspace --all-targets -j 2 -- -D warnings` passed. Both used a private
release target, two test threads and muted audio. The physical CreateRef
regression now exercises the actual in-world focus transition and a real
`Play::frame` consumer after each physical key event, asserting the exact
one-shot InfoPacket request on release and none on press, duplicate release
or focus-loss cancellation. No live login, desktop input or real-window
acceptance was attempted.

**UNRESOLVED, not claimed fixed: MP_Ref_Icon/eighth inspect-cursor mode.**
`InputConfig::FrameProcess` **0x1001ae14** checks modes **0x25..0x2c** and adds
LOOKAT while Shift is held outside HelpMode; this includes reference mode
**0x26**, but does not prove that Knowledge activates it. Fresh caller traces
of `SetNonStaticInputMode` **0x10019b2e** find `NewGuiSpot::CheckHotSpot`
**0x1001d7f7** (mode0x3f) and `MouseDetectorButton::CheckHotSpot`
**0x1001f915** (variable mode). The latter's only constructor caller is legacy
`ActionView_t` **0x100247cf** (mode2). `SetStaticInputMode`
**0x10019b10** callers and scalar searches for **0x26** and direct flag
offset **0x4a** reveal no Knowledge producer. The previous comment that
Inventory implemented `MP_Ref_Icon` was false and is removed; no guessed
Knowledge cursor activation is installed. Grid column-change reflow retains
the existing inventory PositionMap rule, whose exact native ordering is
still unresolved (`inv_grid.rs`, GUI `RecalcCellCount` **0x101349f6**).
